### Page 71

Australian GDP growth is expected
to remain below our estimate of
potential growth throughout the
forecast period.
GDP growth has slowed since the end of 2025 and is
expected to remain subdued over the next couple of
years (Graph 3.3). The expected slowing in growth over
2026 reflects the effects of several headwinds weighing
on private activity, including the reduction in household
disposable incomes from higher inflation, weaker
conditions in the established housing market, and the

tightening in monetary policy, which more than offset
very strong data centre-related investment. Growth is
expected to remain below potential over the forecast
period, reflecting an assumption that financial
conditions remain restrictive (see Key judgement #2).
Consistent with the latest state and federal budgets,
public demand is expected to continue supporting
growth over the forecast period.
The risks to activity appear to be broadly balanced.

Stronger-than-expected global growth and further
strength in AI and data centre-related activity pose
upside risks to domestic growth (see Key risk #2).
However, historically low consumer and business
sentiment could be pointing to the risk that the
combined effect on domestic activity of the Middle East
conflict, the decline in housing prices and the tightening
in monetary policy could be more than assumed (see
Key risk #3).
The level of GDP at end-2028 is expected to be

broadly unchanged from the August forecasts.
Growth is projected to be slightly stronger than
expected in the near term, reflecting a much stronger
outlook for data centre-related investment compared
with the August Statement. Weighing against that is the
higher assumed market path for the cash rate, a weaker
outlook for the housing market and higher oil prices.
These pull growth in the latter part of the forecast period
a little below that expected in the August Statement.

Business investment is expected to increase strongly
and the outlook has been revised up significantly,

largely driven by data centre-related investment.
Business investment was stronger than expected in the
June quarter and firms’ surveyed expectations of future
investment were again revised higher. External
projections for the expected increase in Australia’s data
centre capacity through to 2030 have also continued to
be revised up as the size of the development pipeline
has increased. Our forecast for data centre investment is
in line with recent baseline external projections, but

below projections that assume Australia becomes a
significant regional hub for data centres. There is
considerable uncertainty around the extent of data
centre investment over coming years. This will depend
on a range of factors such as government policy,
community expectations, future demand for AI services
and grid capacity. We continue to expect that much of
this additional investment will be imported (around
70 per cent), limiting the boost to GDP growth. However,

data centre construction, as well as any potential
spillovers to higher investment in energy and utilities
infrastructure, will require domestically sourced labour
and materials. At this stage, most of this investment
pipeline is in New South Wales and Victoria where liaison
information suggests capacity constraints in the
construction sector are somewhat less binding.
3.3 Domestic outlook

### Page 72

At the same time, a higher assumed cash rate path, a
more significant easing in the established housing
market and higher global oil prices are expected to
weigh more on activity than in the August forecasts.
The forecasts are conditioned on a higher market path
for interest rates relative to August. In addition,
established housing prices have continued to fall more
quickly than expected, with external forecasters now
expecting a deeper downturn than assumed a few

months ago. The outlook for oil prices is also much
higher than assumed in August, which will weigh on real
household incomes. Reflecting these factors, the outlook
for household consumption and dwelling investment
has been revised lower, as has the outlook for business
investment unrelated to data centres.
The labour market is expected to
continue easing over the forecast
period, consistent with
below-potential GDP growth.
The unemployment rate is forecast to increase to

5.1 per cent by the end of 2028, which is around
1⁄4 percentage point higher than previously
expected (Graph 3.4). The unemployment rate has risen
by more than expected over recent months, and the
upward revision to forecast relative to the August
Statement largely reflects this higher starting point.
Elevated cost-of-living pressures appear to have
encouraged workers to enter or remain in the labour
force to a greater extent in recent months than was

expected at the time of the August Statement. These
pressures are expected to continue to support
participation for some time. The participation rate is
expected to decline very modestly by the end of 2028 as
easing cyclical pressures are expected to more than
offset the longer-run upward trend in female
participation. A slightly weaker outlook for demand later
in the forecast period is also contributing a little to the
upward revision to the unemployment rate profile by

late 2028.

As in previous forecasts, the
employment-to-population ratio is expected to
decline through to late 2028. Employment growth is

expected to remain broadly stable over the next year, in
line with the leading indicators of labour demand. From
mid-2027, employment growth is expected to be a little
weaker than in the August Statement, reflecting the
weaker outlook for GDP growth later in the forecast
period. The forecast employment-to-population ratio is
similar to the August Statement in the near term, before
declining more quickly to reach a lower level by the end
of the forecast period.

Reserve Bank of A ustralia  |  Statement on Monetar y Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026 69

### Page 73

The economy is expected to move
into balance in 2027, with spare
capacity then emerging in the
economy over the rest of the forecast
period.
Below-potential output growth and an easing in
labour market conditions are expected to see the
economy move from a position of excess demand to
around balance in 2027, before some spare capacity
emerges. The timing of the return to balance is slightly
later than in the August Statement, as a result of the
upgrade to near-term GDP growth.

However, there is considerable uncertainty around
the assessment of when the economy will return to
balance and the extent of spare capacity that
emerges. The impact of conflict-related costs on
inflation over the period ahead will also make it more
difficult to assess the degree of underlying capacity
pressures in the domestic economy. Our assumption for
potential output growth over the next couple of years
assumes productivity growth will pick up gradually (see

Key risk #4). However, with the level of labour
productivity remaining around the same rate as prior to
the pandemic – implying near-zero productivity growth
on average over that period – continued weak
productivity outcomes may be constraining the ability of
firms to grow their supply capacity without generating
inflationary pressure.
Wages growth is expected to pick up
a little in the near term, then
moderate gradually from 2027 as
labour market conditions ease.

The forecast for year-ended wages growth has been
revised a little higher across the forecast period. In
the near term, nominal wages growth is expected to be
supported by the effect of higher-than-expected
capacity pressures in the first half of the year as well as
the Fair Work Commission’s award wage decision (which
had already been incorporated in the August forecasts).
The influence of both higher-than-expected inflation
outcomes and inflation expectations on wage

negotiations is expected to add further upward pressure
on wages growth relative to the August forecast. Some
large wage increases in enterprise bargaining
agreements are expected to support wages growth in
the near term, although the timing of pay increases for
these agreements may contribute to volatility in
quarterly outcomes, particularly for the public sector.
Wages growth expectations from firms in the RBA’s
liaison program are elevated and suggest little

easing over the year ahead (see Box C: Insights from
Liaison). This is consistent with the central forecast for
wages growth. Growth is expected to moderate from
mid-2027 onwards, reflecting easing labour market
conditions and lower inflation outcomes. Wages growth
is expected to have eased to 2.9 per cent in year-ended
terms by end-2028 (Graph 3.5).

Unit labour cost growth is forecast to
be elevated in the near term, before
easing over the second half of the
forecast period.
Year-ended growth in unit labour costs (ULCs) is
expected to increase in coming quarters, reflecting
weak productivity growth and higher wages growth.
Year-ended growth in ULCs is then expected to
moderate from late 2027 onwards, alongside slowing
nominal wages growth and the pick-up in productivity
growth to its assumed medium-term trend growth rate

of 0.7 per cent per year (Graph 3.6). Nominal ULCs are
the measure of labour costs most relevant for firms’ cost
of production and so for inflation outcomes. Year-ended
ULCs growth is expected to remain above 2.5 per cent –
the rate that is consistent with inflation being
sustainably at target – until late in the forecast period.

The forecast for underlying inflation
has been revised higher over the
next year, mainly owing to higher
conflict-related cost pressures and
higher-than-expected domestic
capacity pressures.
Trimmed mean inflation is expected to remain
above 3 per cent until the end of 2027, easing
gradually to reach 21⁄2 per cent around mid-2028
(Graph 3.7). Year-ended underlying inflation is expected

to remain elevated at a little above 31⁄2 per cent until
mid-2027, reflecting existing capacity pressures in the
economy and the pass-through of higher
conflict-related costs to consumer prices. Elevated
inflation expectations are also contributing to
underlying inflation over the coming year or so. From
mid-2027 onwards, inflation is expected to ease due to
the emergence of spare capacity in the economy and
labour market and as conflict-related cost effects begin

to unwind.

### Page 75

Cost pressures related to the Middle East conflict are
expected to place more upward pressure on
underlying inflation over the year ahead than
previously assumed. A possible re-escalation in the
conflict had been flagged as an upside risk to inflation in
the August Statement. Since then, oil and fuel prices
have increased by much more than had been assumed
and are now assumed to remain at a higher level over
the forecast period. We expect higher global energy

prices will contribute to higher domestic inflationary
pressures in late 2026 and early 2027, even as domestic
activity slows and the labour market eases (Graph 3.8).
The judgement around the degree of pass-through from
global energy prices to domestic prices is broadly similar
to what had been assumed in August. It is consistent
with information from liaison and the Business Outlook
Scenario Survey (BOSS) in September, where firms
reported plans to continue raising prices in response to

past cost increases arising partially from the conflict.
From mid-2027 onwards, we expect cost effects to
unwind in net terms, albeit to a lesser extent than
assumed in the August Statement as oil prices are now
assumed to remain at a higher level than previously
expected and refined margins are assumed to remain
elevated for longer (see Key judgement #1). The balance
of risks to conflict-related inflationary pressures remains
skewed to the upside, particularly as crude oil and

refined fuel prices could remain elevated or rise further
(see Key risk #1).
Relative to the August forecasts, domestic capacity
pressures are also expected to exert a bit more
upward pressure on inflation over the year ahead.
We now judge that stronger-than-expected domestic
capacity pressures explain more of the recent
unexpected strength in underlying inflation (see
Chapter 2: Economic Conditions). Those higher capacity
pressures are also expected to contribute a little more to

inflation over the year ahead. This upward pressure is
expected to ease gradually over the forecast period, as
spare capacity emerges in the economy and the
labour market.

Elevated inflation expectations are expected to

contribute to inflationary pressure over the forecast
period, supporting the pass-through of firms’ costs
to consumer prices. Several measures of short- and
medium-term inflation expectations have increased
alongside the recent increases in fuel prices, and some
measures of longer-term expectations have also drifted
higher (see Chapter 2: Economic Conditions). We judge
that elevated expectations will contribute to higher
realised inflation by supporting expected cost growth,

making firms more willing to pass these higher costs
through to prices, and placing upward pressure on
wages growth. If the conflict in the Middle East is more
protracted than assumed in our baseline forecasts, there
is a risk that inflation expectations may increase further
(see Key risk #1).

Headline inflation has been revised
higher in the near term due to
stronger growth in fuel prices and
higher underlying inflation.
Headline inflation is expected to remain above 3 per
cent until late 2027, which is around three quarters
longer than in the August forecast, and reach the
midpoint of the 2–3 per cent inflation target range a
bit later than expected in August (Graph 3.9). Headline
inflation is expected to peak at 4.5 per cent in the
December quarter this year due to

higher-than-expected fuel prices. The assumed decline
in global oil prices is expected to put downward
pressure on headline inflation from early 2027. Over the
remainder of the forecast period, headline inflation has
been revised higher relative to our August forecasts,
reflecting higher underlying inflation (Graph 3.10).
Headline inflation is expected to reach the midpoint of
the target in mid-2028 as capacity pressures and
conflict-related costs ease.

### Page 77

The staff assess that the risks to the domestic inflation
outlook are skewed to the upside, despite becoming
more balanced since the August Statement as some of
the risks identified then have been incorporated into the
central forecast. In August, we identified three main
sources of upside risk to the global and domestic
inflation outlook: a re-escalation of the Middle East
conflict; domestic capacity pressures being greater than
assumed; and the global AI boom generating more of an

inflationary impetus than assumed. Since then, the risk
of a re-escalation of the Middle East conflict has
crystallised and staff have re-evaluated the extent of
domestic capacity constraints; both of which have led to
upward revisions to the central inflation forecast over the
next year. However, there is still uncertainty around the
outlook for global energy prices, the role of inflation
expectations in price and wage dynamics as well as the
impact on inflation from AI activity.

The staff also identified in August that there were upside
risks to global and domestic economic activity from
AI-related demand, but that domestic economic activity
could be weaker if the Middle East conflict and the
decline in housing prices affected the economy by more
than assumed in the central forecast. These remain key
risks to the outlook.
Key risk #1 – High inflation could be
more persistent if energy prices
remain elevated, or if inflation

expectations increase or start to
have a greater bearing on wage- and
price-setting.
We have modelled a scenario where crude oil
remains around US$100 per barrel and refined
product prices remain at elevated levels over the
forecast period. Many external forecasters are revising
higher their outlook for crude oil and refined product
prices, on concerns that some of the key margins of
adjustment in global energy markets that have helped
to cushion the impact of the disruption to oil supply and

global activity are reaching their limits (see Chapter 2:
Economic Conditions and Key judgement #1).
Persistently higher global oil prices would leave
domestic inflation higher for a longer period than in
the central forecast. Higher oil prices would directly
increase fuel prices and firms’ input costs, with the latter
gradually passed through to consumer prices as firms
adjust to persistently higher costs. By contrast, the
central forecast assumes a decline in oil prices, which

starts to ease cost pressures related to the Middle East
conflict from mid-2027, contributing to lower inflation
over time (Graph 3.11). Higher inflation also raises
inflation expectations in the scenario, reinforcing
inflationary pressures over the forecast period. Assuming
that the cash rate and exchange rate follow the same
path that underpins the central forecasts, underlying
inflation would be around 23⁄4 per cent by
end-2028 rather than being around the 21⁄2 per cent.

3.4 Key risks to the outlook
Chapter 3  |  OutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlook
Reserve Bank of A ustralia  |  Statement on Monetar y Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026 74

### Page 78

It is possible that inflation expectations could rise
even further or that inflation expectations provide
greater impetus to wages growth and inflation
through second-round effects. The central forecasts
and scenario assume that the increase in households’
and firms’ inflation expectations do exert some upward
pressure on inflation and wages growth. However, given
households and firms are paying particularly close
attention to inflation, it is possible that inflation

expectations will influence price- and wage-setting
decisions by more than assumed. This heightened
attentiveness to inflation could also make expectations
more persistent and more responsive to realised
inflation. With headline inflation forecast to rise further in
the near term, this increases the risk that inflation
expectations remain higher for longer than assumed in
our central forecast.
Key risk #2 – The global AI demand
boom may continue to drive up

global growth and inflation.
MTP growth may not moderate as expected over the
next few years if AI-demand growth continues at the
same pace. It is possible that growth forecasts could
continue to be upwardly revised by Consensus
forecasters if the current upswing in AI-related
investment (and spillovers associated with the boom) is
more persistent than currently assumed. The rise in
interest rates across the yield curve in many advanced
economies may reflect higher neutral rates – that is,

expectations that demand will prove more resilient and
inflation more persistent than currently assumed. Higher
longer-term real rates may partly reflect expectations of
stronger future productivity growth, including from the
adoption of AI. Further investment in anticipation of
higher productivity could also support demand and add
to capacity pressures over the next few years, while most
of the productivity gains are likely to manifest beyond
the next two years (at least in Australia).

The ramp-up of investment in data centres may also
contribute to capacity and pricing pressures
domestically, particularly by drawing on resources in
the construction industry. These price pressures could
be stronger than currently expected as the forecast
pipeline of investment is worked through, or if
investment proves stronger than expected.
Key risk #3 – Domestic economic
activity could be weaker than
expected.
It is possible that the headwinds affecting

households and businesses over the next year could
have a larger adverse effect than assumed. The
central forecasts assume that higher oil prices, higher
interest rates and lower housing prices all weigh on
domestic economic activity broadly in line with average
historical relationships. Timely indicators of spending
remain consistent with this assumption, with no
evidence of a material slowing in consumption growth
in recent months. However, the very weak levels of

consumer and business sentiment could be pointing to
a more pronounced slowing in household and business
spending growth than assumed.
We have calibrated a downside domestic activity
scenario where there is a larger response in
household and business spending to these
headwinds. There is a range of estimates for the effects
of these headwinds in the literature, highlighting the
uncertainty around their impact on economic activity.
Drawing on this evidence, the scenario uses larger

responses than assumed in the central forecast, thereby
identifying a plausible downside outlook for domestic
activity. In the scenario, firms feel forced to pass on the
conflict-related costs to prices as in the central forecast;
but households respond more cautiously to the
resultant pressures on real income and wealth, reducing
discretionary consumption by more than assumed in the
central forecast. Such an effect on spending might be
compounded by a tightening in borrowing constraints

and incentives for housing investors. The scenario
assumes firms respond to weaker demand and margin
pressure with a sharper pullback on (non-data centre)
investment and hiring. Overall, the level of GDP is around
1.0 per cent lower than the central forecasts
by end-2028.
The larger pullback in household and business
spending results in weaker activity and employment
growth, but only modestly lower inflation than in
the central forecast, assuming interest rates and the

exchange rate follow the same path as the central
forecast. The greater caution exhibited by households
pushes the gross household saving ratio up gradually to
a peak of around 17 per cent, where it remains over the
remainder of the forecast period. The greater reduction
Chapter 3  |  OutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlook

### Page 79

in hiring pushes the unemployment rate 0.3 percentage
points higher by end-2028, creating more spare capacity
in the domestic economy than in the central forecast
(Graph 3.12). The additional spare capacity in the
economy and the labour market reduces inflationary
pressures somewhat, but the assumption of cost
pass-through by firms limits this effect. Overall, trimmed
mean inflation reaches 21⁄2 per cent only one quarter
earlier than in the central forecast.

Key risk #4 – There remains
considerable uncertainty about the
degree of capacity pressures in the
domestic economy and labour
market.
Despite the recent reassessment, it is possible that

we are still understating the extent of capacity
pressures. While we assess that capacity constraints in
the economy and labour market have been tighter than
previously judged, model-based estimates of the
unemployment rate that is consistent with low and
stable inflation continue to point to greater tightness in
labour market conditions than our central assessment. In
addition, if productivity growth were to remain subdued
over the forecast period, rather than pick up modestly as

assumed, it may suggest that the economy’s supply
capacity would be lower than currently expected. The
implications for inflation would depend on how
households and businesses adjust their spending
and expectations.
Conversely, capacity pressures may be weaker than
expected. The broader suite of full employment
indicators points to somewhat less labour market
tightness than our central assessment. It is also possible
that more of the recent strength in wages growth and

inflation reflects factors other than excess demand, such
as conflict-related cost effects or higher inflation
expectations. In these cases, wages growth and inflation
may moderate more quickly than in the central forecast.
Productivity growth could also recover more quickly
than assumed, including if the anticipated benefits of AI
adoption and other technological developments are
realised sooner.
There is also a risk that the unemployment rate
increases by more than expected if labour supply

remains stronger than assumed. There is a risk that
elevated cost-of-living pressures could support labour
force participation for longer, or to a greater extent, than
assumed. If so, stronger labour supply could result in a
higher unemployment rate than forecast, even if labour
demand evolves broadly as expected.

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -49.02 điểm tương ứng -2.66%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dệt may, Y tế và Bất động sản là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -4,944 tỷ đồng, 
tập trung ở các mã VPB, TCB và VHM; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng ở mức 1995 
tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại bán ròng -5,280 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua và đang 
duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế 3,630 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 208 hợp đồng nhưng đang 
duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,428 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam tiếp tục điều chỉnh tuần thứ hai liên tiếp sau nâng hạng, khi VN-Index lùi 
về đóng cửa 1.737 điểm — giảm khoảng 2% so với tuần trước và đang kiểm định lại vùng 
đáy cũ của tháng 8, quanh 1.720. Áp lực bán duy trì khi chỉ số giảm nhiều phiên liên tiếp, 
cho thấy hiệu ứng "mua kỳ vọng, bán thực tế" sau nâng hạng vẫn chưa kết thúc, trong bối 
cảnh dòng tiền thận trọng và thanh khoản ở mức thấp. Khối ngoại tiếp tục là lực cản 
chính khi bán ròng mạnh cả 5 phiên trong tuần, riêng phiên 2/10 bán ròng tới 3.361 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở PNJ và HDB. Áp lực bên ngoài vẫn hiện diện khi Fed duy trì lập trường 
diều hâu và giá dầu ở mức cao; trong một, hai tuần tới, mùa báo cáo KQKD sẽ bắt đầu có 
tác động, kỳ vọng theo chiều hướng tích cực, qua đó giúp nâng đỡ thị trường chung.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành cây nến giảm điểm tiêu cực trên khung tuần, xuyên thủng vùng hỗ trợ 
gần quanh 1750, cho thấy áp lực bán đã có phần quyết liệt hơn sau 2 tuần giằng co trước 
đó. Trên khung ngày, sau các phiên lình xình vào đầu tuần, chỉ số cũng dần chuyển sang 
diễn biến giảm điểm rõ nét hơn về cuối tuần, đồng thời cắt sâu xuống dưới SMA50, thể 
hiện áp lực phân phối đang có phần lấn át mặc dù chưa xảy ra hiện tượng bán tháo khi 
khối lượng khớp lệnh được giữ ở mức thấp. Diễn biến tính từ vùng đỉnh 1870 vào đầu tháng 
9 đang không mấy khả quan, tuy nhiên xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn, tính từ đáy 1640 vào cuối 
tháng 7, hiện vẫn đang được bảo lưu và VNINDEX được kỳ vọng sẽ có nhịp hồi phục trở lại 
khi về đến vùng hỗ trợ kế tiếp tại quanh 1710. 
Kháng cự xa: 1820 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1775 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1710 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1670 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

Document 319: Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

By Advanced Economies Team
Advanced Economies:
Resilience to shock after shock
October 2026

### Page 2

• The global economy is in the midst of a growth cycle. As a result, GDP growth is more resilient to shocks than initially
anticipated.
• The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise in oil prices, whose negative impact on growth is offset
by a series of reflationary forces.
• The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial intelligence (AI).
• The world’s major economies are currently engaged in a broader push to expand their capital goods investment.

• While European and US households are experiencing more or less the same energy price shock, inflation and household
consumption remain higher in the US.
• Higher and above-target inflation calls for monetary tightening.
• Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks examined in this publication (the US, the UK, the
Eurozone). Inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026 or Q1 2027, but there is a significant risk that energy inflation will

spread further to other components of inflation in the future.
• Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength (in the US).
• Monetary policies also have to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal policy (particularly in Japan).
• The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: the ensuing rise in energy prices contributes to the recent increase in
long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the start of the year) to levels close to those of 2002.

• The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period of growth), reflecting different fiscal trajectories. In the
US, the UK, France and Germany the public debt-to-GDP ratio is increasing, while it has stabilized in Italy and decreased in
Spain and Japan.
• The rise in long-term interest rates will weigh on public finances. The fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it will be in 2030.
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with GDP growth.

For more information, please refer to our publication:
EcoPerspectives – Advanced Economies | 3rd quarter of 2026, as of September 24, 2026– Economic Research– BNP Paribas
Advanced economies: Resilience to shock after shock

Document 323: Cliff Notes_ the source and persistence of inflation (2026-11-05)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ the source and persistence of inflation (2026-11-05).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-05

Status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: the source and persistence of inflation
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
11:16 October 30 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia this week, all eyes were on the September CPI. Headline inflation rose 0.8% (4.3%yr) in
the month, largely owing to a 9.4% jump in fuel prices and a seasonal surge in domestic and
international holiday travel & accommodation. The underlying inflation pulse also proved hotter than

anticipated for Q3 overall, up 1.0% (3.7%yr).

### Page 2

While some parts of the basket are seeing disinflation – housing in particular looks to be cooling faster
than expected as builders lure sales with discounts – other components are still showing persistence.
Highlighting this is market services ex volatiles, which the RBA watches closely as an indicator of price
pressures related to domestic capacity. In Q3, this metric rose 1.2% (3.2%yr), and the monthly detail
points to momentum holding up – in line with our expectations for another chunky read in Q4.

With inflation running well above the RBA’s August forecasts, and some of the energy risks flagged
then materialising, we continue to expect the Monetary Policy Board will deliver a 25bp rate hike to
4.85% in November. Beyond November, another rate hike in February is a risk, but is not currently part
of our base case.
Across the Tasman, the RBNZ this week surprised market expectations for a 25bp rate hike, deciding
instead to wait for further information before taking the next step with policy. The decision was made

4:3, with the Governor in the ‘on hold’ camp. A 25bp hike in December to 3.00% and further tightening
in 2027 (Westpac NZ forecasts a peak of 4.00% in September) seems probable; however, each
decision will depend on fuel prices, inflation expectations and evidence of second-round pricing
pressures, along with the evolution of global economic risks.
In the US, the FOMC also held rates steady at its October meeting. Based on prior comments by key
members, the decision was not a surprise, and neither were Governor Cook and Cleveland Fed

President Hammack’s dissents in favour of a 25bp hike. The Committee remains positive on economic
activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding at a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in
September). Chair Warsh also subsequently emphasised the breadth of economic growth outside of
tech-related investment (evinced by Q3 GDP the day after; see below).
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in the statement. However, inflation “remains

elevated” and the FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable return of annual inflation
to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target. This guidance and the “accumulation of more information will help
the Committee determine the policy stance” suggests another 25bp hike is likely in December.
Thereafter, the FOMC will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related risks through
2027; although, in the absence of another shock, we expect them to remain on hold.

The Q3 GDP report certainly supported Chair Warsh’s view on the breadth of growth, the 3.4%
annualised gain for GDP benefitting from a 3.8% annualised increase in household consumption,
12.7% growth in non-residential investment as well as a robust increase in government spending.
Notably though, the contribution from business investment was effectively offset by net exports’ drag
given most of the componentry related to AI infrastructure investment is imported. This is not to say that

US firms won’t benefit from the investment once complete, but simply that much of the initial return
goes to the foreign businesses who provide the necessary capital equipment.
On consumption, the monthly PCE data implies downside risks remain for the consumer, the increase
in nominal spending seen in the month requiring dissaving and, on a real basis, consumption flat in the
month following a 0.5% gain in August. Cost-of-living pressures and uncertainty over the outlook is

### Page 3

Related articles
expected to see household consumption growth average around trend over the coming year, albeit with
considerable volatility quarter to quarter. This should see PCE inflation slow back towards its 2.0%yr
medium-term target as the FOMC desires, from 3.6%yr and 3.1%yr for the headline and core
measures respectively as of September.
Across the Atlantic, the ECB also left its key policy rates unchanged in October. Of note from the
meeting communications, energy prices continue to pose upside risks for inflation, but also downside

risks for growth. "While the full effects of the energy shock are still unfolding, underlying inflation has
remained broadly stable so far and wages do not show a material response". "Financing conditions
have [also] tightened", "which is likely to weigh on inflation and growth". In the press conference,
President Lagarde noted that, between the October and December meetings, a significant amount of
new economic information will be released, allowing forecasts and risks assessments to be tested.

Based on the October meeting communications, we continue to expect a rate hike at the December
meeting but believe this will be prove to be the last hike of the cycle. Note though, the spectrum of risks
Europe faces is broad and skewed towards inflation in the near term, hence the Council’s guidance will
remain hawkish. President Lagarde also announced today that she will step down as ECB President in
February ahead of the scheduled conclusion of her term next October.

Date: 2026-10-26

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.39 / 26 October 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
In brief. The week was dominated by a continued global selloff in tech
stocks, leading to safe haven flows into US Treasuries, Japanese Go -
vernment bonds and German bunds. As a result, their yields fell des -
pite energy prices surging to or past their Spring highs. Economic data
generally showed continued resilience. French bonds were under rising

pressure, but Moody's unexpected decision to maintain its France ra -
ting unchanged could, at the margin, help stabilise the spread. The Fed,
ECB and BOJ should be on hold next week while signaling further rate
action is likely.
EUROPEAN UNION
A win for securitization. The Council and the European Parliament have
reached a provisional political agreement on measures to revive and
simplify the EU’s securitization market. The updated framework will

reduce banks’ capital requirements for the safest and most well-struc-
tured transactions, notably for a newly introduced “resilient” category
of securitization. As a key component of the “One Europe, One Market”
initiative and the EU’s Savings and Investment Union agenda, this aims
to channel increased lending to households and businesses. The rele -
vant legal texts must now be finalised.
EUROZONE
Expansion continues. The flash PMIs weakened in October, mainly on

the back of a decline in France. The EZ composite index dipped to 51.9
(-1.2 m/m), dragged down by services (51.7, -1.3 m/m), although ma -
nufacturing also dipped (52.4, -0.5 m/m). Both remain in expansion
territory, and manufacturing new export orders rose to their highest
level since early 2022. Price signals were also rather reassuring, with
the services output price index easing to 54.9 (-1.7 m/m). Households
are more cautious: consumer confidence fell for a second consecutive

month to a five-month low (-17.9). Households’ inflation expectations
edged up at short horizons (+0.1 pp to 3.6% at one year and 3.1% at
three years), but long-term expectations remained anchored, with the
five-year median stable at 2.5%.
France
Rising market stress despite decent economic news; a reprieve from
Moody’s. The spread on French 10-year bond relative to Germany’s
peaked to 174 bps on Friday, the highest since 2011 and only 16 bps
short of its all-time high, as markets price in anticipation of worst-case

scenarios regarding the country’s ability to repair its public finances.
The 10Y yield briefly exceeded 5.1% before falling back to 5% (above
the US equivalent). Nonetheless, services output was stable in August,
following a 0.6% m/m increase in July, a much higher pace than in Q2.
The rebound is driven by the catering sector, the digital sector, business
services, and real estate. The INSEE business climate improved from
96 to 97 (long-term average = 100) in October, but the survey was

conducted before the riots. The main improvement was in manufactu-
ring, which rose from 101 to 105, driven by increased new orders in the
aeronautics sector, and to a lesser extent by the services sector (from
98 to 99). The PMI survey (conducted partially after the riots) was
less positive: the composite index fell to 48.4 (-2.7 m/m), with a sharp
decline in services (48.4; -2.8 m/m) and a slightly smaller decline in
manufacturing (49.3; -1.3 m/m). Moody's unexpectedly announced af -

ter the market close on Friday that it was maintaining France’s ra -
ting at Aa3 on account of France’s diversified economic structure and
sound public institutions, but maintained its negative outlook. This is
on par with the UK and one notch above S&P and Fitch. Moody’s made
clear keeping this rating is conditional on a budget being adopted that
reduces the fiscal deficit, along with an easing of social and market
stress going forward. Saturday’s demonstrations across the country

saw strong attendance but only limited violent incidents.
Germany
Prices continue to rise, but confidence surveys remain on an upward
trend. Producer prices rose 6.7% y/y in September (+2.1pp m/m; the
highest level in 3.5 years) due to higher prices for energy and inter -
mediate goods, primarily metals. The ZEW Economic Sentiment Index
improved beyond consensus forecasts in October and returned to its
highest level since May 2023 (-35.9; +11.2 m/m), albeit the Economic

Expectations Index fell to its lowest level since July (28.1; -6.6 m/m).
The recovery in economic activity continues, although a slowdown is
evident: the flash Composite PMI slowed (52.8; -1 m/m; the lowest in
2 months) due to weaker growth in the services sector (51.7; -1.1 m/m;
the lowest in 2 months) and in manufacturing output (55.1; -0.9 m/m;
the lowest in 3 months). Nevertheless, in both sectors, hiring is rising
slightly, supported by growth in export sales and the spillover effects of

investment plans. Despite persistently high input prices, selling prices
are slowing (reaching their lowest level since February in the manu -
facturing sector).
UNITED KINGDOM
Inflation, wage growth and activity accelerate. CPI inflation overshot
consensus on energy prices, printing at 0.4% m/m and 3.5% y/y in Sep-
tember (consensus: 3.4%, prior: 3.1%), its highest in 11 months. Core
CPI came in at 2.8% y/y (up 0.2 pp), in line with consensus. Pressures

remained persistent according to the PPI at 8.3% y/y (prior: 6.7%). La -
bour market and activity data also surprised positively. Payroll growth
reached 102k in August (3m/3m), bringing the unemployment rate to
4.7% (down 0.2 pp), its lowest since July 2025. October PMI surveys
beat expectations in both manufacturing (52.8, consensus: 51.8, prior:
51.9) and services (53.1, consensus: 51.8, prior: 52.1). The only negative
news came from headline retail sales, falling 0.4% m/m in September,

with the core gauge (excluding petrol) deteriorating by -0.7% m/m.
UNITED STATES
Construction struggling, GENIUS Act implemented. Housing starts
dropped -5.2% m/m in September (-7.4% y/y). This third consecutive
monthly decline dragged the Q3 average to its lowest since Q2 2020.
Building permits also came in negative, at -0.2% m/m (-3.0% y/y), while
the NAHB homebuilder sentiment index plunged to its weakest since
2012. The average 30-year fixed mortgage rate eased to 7.32% (still

134bp above pre-war level), as the ongoing AI selloff pushed down
US treasury yields. The GENIUS Act final implementation rule from the
Office of the Comptroller of the Currency’s (OCC) leaves the door open
for stablecoin customers to receive yield or rewards. Issuers remain
prohibited from paying holders any form of interest or yield solely
in connection with the holding, use, or retention of payment stable -
coins. This represents a shift from the OCC’s February proposal and

has renewed concerns within the banking industry about deposit flight.
The rule will take effect on January 18, 2027.
JAPAN
Activity and inflation keep rising. Composite PMI was unchanged in
September at 52.3, solidly in expansion territory, as an improvement
in services activity offset slower output growth in manufacturing. The
Manufacturing PMI disappointed at 53.3 (-0.8 m/m; an 8-month low).
Underlying price pressures, meanwhile, extended. Headline CPI was

flat on a monthly basis, bringing the annual rate up to 2.1% (up 0.4 pp).
Core CPI (excl. fresh food) matched it at 2.1% y/y (up 0.4 pp), in line
with consensus. More importantly, the New Core index, which strips
out fresh food and energy, rose sharply to 2.4% y/y (up 0.5 pp), its
fastest pace since January.
EMERGING ECONOMIES

ASIA
China
New monetary policy tool and export controls. The central bank’s Go-
vernor Pan Gongsheng unveiled a plan to switch (“when appropriate”)
its key policy rate from the 7-day reverse repo rate (at 1.4% since May
2025) to the overnight reverse repo rate, in order to reflect develop -
ments in the interbank market. New export controls: The government
plans to add 40 key technologies to the list of technologies prohibited
or restricted for export, including in the AI (for ex. LLMs, computing

clusters), biotechnology and photovoltaic sectors.
Indonesia
BI kept policy rate unchanged at 5.75%, raised its deposit facility rate
by 25bp to 5% and announced a phased cut in reserve requirements
from 9% to 7% by December. This combination of limited FX support
and measures to support credit growth sends mixed signals amidst
pressures on the rupiah following MSCI’s downgrade warning and un-
dermines the central bank’s credibility. The rupiah has lost 1% against

the USD since mid-October, with little contagion effect on other Asian
currencies.
South Korea
The Bank of Korea (BoK) kept its policy rate unchanged at 3.0%, but
the tightening cycle may not be over. CPI inflation was 2.9% y/y in Sep-
tember, above the central bank’s target of 2%, while high oil prices,
rapid growth in Seoul housing prices and wage pressures (though
concentrated in the electronics sector) raise the risk of lasting infla -
tion pressure. As economic activity remains robust, supported by strong

exports and recovering investment, the BoK has scope to raise rates
further.
EMEA
Hungary
Monetary status quo and 2027 budget in consolidation mode. The
Central Bank kept its policy rate unchanged at 5.5%, as expected. A cau-
tious stance will likely prevail in the short term. Inflation has been
picking up in recent months, albeit mildly, and will continue to move
upward as the government recently removed the margin cap on fuel.
Separately, the Budget for 2027 and the medium-term fiscal plan show

Hungary’s goal to join the Eurozone; the government expects to reduce
the fiscal deficit to 4.9% of GDP in 2027 (from 7% expected in 2026) and
towards 2.8% of GDP by 2030.
Türkiye
Cautious monetary loosening. The Central Bank lowered its policy rate
by 100 bp to 36% at its October Monetary Policy Committee meeting,
in line with market expectations. The overnight lending and borrowing
rates were also reduced by 100 bp, to 39% and 34.5%, respectively.

The improvement in underlying inflation has yet to translate into a
sustained period of low inflation, justifying the CBRT’s preference for a
measured pace of monetary easing.
LATIN AMERICA
Brazil
President Lula da Silva was re-elected on Sunday for a fourth term
(with 51.8% of the vote), defeating far right-wing candidate Flavio Bol-
sonaro (48.2%). Lula will enter this new term facing significant econo -
mic and political challenges. Public debt has risen to more than 82% of

GDP , while high borrowing costs and rigid mandatory spending leave
limited room to reconcile fiscal consolidation with social spending and
investment. Restoring confidence in the fiscal outlook will be a key
challenge in the short term.
COMMODITIES
Pressure rising. Amid growing rumours of imminent re-escalation of
military operations in the Gulf, energy prices rose sharply this week,
with brent touching $117/b – near its April peak, and European gas

prices (TTF) rising to 97 €/MWh on Monday, their highest level since
Dec 2022. The Asian gas benchmark (JKM) is following the same trend
(4.6% w/w to 31.4 $/mbtu, equivalent to around 96 €/MWh). Diesel
crack spreads are slightly decreasing in Europe (-2.8% w/w on Monday)
and in the US (-4% w/w on Monday). The first Russian diesel tanker
intended to the US market left Russia this Friday and should supply the
US market in 2 weeks. US diesel average retail prices remain oriented

to the upside (0.7% w/w at 6.32 $/gallon on Monday). US commercial
crude inventories fell by 3.1 mb in the week ending 16 October, signi -
ficantly exceeding market expectations for a 340,000-barrel decline.

Document 414: RBAは２回連続の利上げ決定も、景気減速懸念で追加利上げには慎重姿勢 ～インフレ圧力の根強さが利上げを促すも、景気下振れ懸念_d8ef4396

Source PDF: all report pdf/RBAは２回連続の利上げ決定も、景気減速懸念で追加利上げには慎重姿勢 ～インフレ圧力の根強さが利上げを促すも、景気下振れ懸念_d8ef4396.pdf

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年 11 月 4 日

1/6 
RBA は２回連続の利上げ決定も、景気減速懸念で追加利上げには慎重姿勢 
～インフレ圧力の根強さが利上げを促すも、景気下振れ懸念や追加利上げ期待の後退が豪ドルの重しに～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ オーストラリア準備銀行（ＲＢＡ）は、11 月２～３日に開催した定例会合において、政策金
利を 25bp 引き上げて 4.85％とすることを決定した。利上げは２会合連続、年明け以降５回
目となり、政策金利は18 年ぶりの高水準となった。 
○ 背景には、原油高に加え、国内の需給ひっ迫を反映してインフレ圧力が根強く、物価上昇が
幅広い財・サービスに波及していることがある。ＲＢＡは物価見通しを大幅に上方修正し、
インフレ率が目標レンジに収束する時期も従来の想定から後ずれすると見込んでいる。 
○ 一方で、今回の利上げ決定においては、理事９人のうち３人が据え置きを支持するなど、景
気減速への警戒感も強まっている。ブロック総裁は、これまでの利上げによる累積的な影響
を見極める必要性を強調しており、ＲＢＡは当面、政策金利を現行水準に据え置き、物価や
雇用、不動産市場の動向を慎重に見極める展開が予想される。ただし、インフレ圧力が高止
まりすれば追加利上げに動く可能性も残ると考えられる。 
○ 豪ドル相場については、利上げが事前に織り込まれていたことに加え、追加利上げ期待の後
退を受けて弱含んだ。先行きについても、国内景気の減速懸念や米国の金融政策、 中国景気、
中東情勢などの影響を受けつつ、当面は上値の重い展開が続く可能性がある。

### Page 2

2/6 
【RBAは２会合連続の利上げ決定、年明け以降５回目、政策金利は18 年ぶりの高水準に】 
オーストラリア準備銀行（ＲＢＡ）は、11 月２～３日の日程で開催した定例の金融政策委員会に
おいて、政策金利であるオフィシャルキ
ャッシュレート（ＯＣＲ）を 25bp 引き上
げて 4.85％とすることを決定した （図
１）。ＲＢＡは、 ２月の定例会合で２年３
ヶ月ぶりの利上げに動き、５月の定例会
合まで３会合連続の利上げを実施した。
その後、６月会合、８月会合では金利を
据え置く様子見姿勢に転じたものの、９
月会合で３会合ぶりの利上げに動いた。
このため、今回は２会合連続、年明け以
降５回目となる。今回の利上げにより、
ＯＣＲは18 年ぶりの水準となった。 
事前の市場予想では、ＲＢＡが利上げに動くとの見方が大宗を占めた。背景には、ＲＢＡ高官か
ら先行きの政策運営について、タカ派姿勢を維持する発言が相次いだことがある。 なかでもハウザ
ー副総裁は、原油高などを理由にインフレ見通しが「著しく悪化している」として、金融政策では
供給ショックに対処できないとしつつ、 コスト上昇がインフレ期待の上昇に波及することを防ぐ必
要があるとの考えを示した。そのうえ、物価動向がＲＢＡの想定を上回る可能性が高いとの見方を
示すなど、 インフレに対する警戒感を強調した。 同氏の発言は政策運営の方向性を明示したもので
はないものの、インフレ見通しの悪化に加え、インフレ期待を抑え込む必要性を強調したことは、
金融市場に追加利上げを意識させるに十分であったと考えられる（注１）。

注１ 10 月21 日付レポート「弱含む動きをみせてきた豪ドル相場は持ち直しに向かうか」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666763.html）

### Page 3

3/6 
７-９月のインフレ率は前年同期比＋3.9％と前期 （同＋3.9％） から横ばいで推移し、 ＲＢＡが定
める目標レンジ（２～３％）を引き続き上回るとともに、ＲＢＡが８月会合で示した見通し（前年
比＋3.6％）をも上回った（図２） 。前期
比も＋1.3％と前期（同＋0.6％）から上
昇ペースが加速し、 食料品やエネルギー
など生活必需品の物価上昇に加え、サー
ビス物価も上昇基調を強める 動きが確
認された。コアインフレ率（トリム平均
値）も前年同期比＋3.7％と前期（同＋
3.6％）からわずかに伸びが加速してお
り、 ＲＢＡの見通し （同＋3.3％） も上回
っ た。前期 比も＋ 1.0％ と前期（ 同＋
0.8％） から上昇ペースが加速しており、
基調的なインフレ圧力が強まっていることがうかがえた。さらに、ＲＢＡのブロック総裁が議会証
言において、 これまでの利上げにより物価が目標に回帰させることが可能か、今回会合で議論する
考えを示すとともに、物価上昇圧力が依然強いとの認識を示した。このため、金融市場では利上げ
が実施されるとの見方が一段と強まった（注２）。

【評決は６対３に割れる、インフレ見通しを上方修正する一方、景気の下振れリスクにも警戒】 
会合後に公表された声明文では、 理事９人のうち６人が利上げに賛成した一方、 ３人は据え置き
を支持するなど、 金融引き締めの必要性を巡って判断が分かれたことが明らかにされた。 前回の９
月会合では、全会一致で利上げを決定したことを踏まえれば、今回の票割れは、インフレ抑制を優
先する姿勢が維持される一方、 景気減速への警戒が理事会内で強まりつつあることを示唆している。 
物価動向について「依然高水準にある」 、 「足元のデータはインフレの広がりを示唆している」と
の認識を示した。 加えて、 「インフレ見通しも大幅に上方修正された」 として、 その理由に 「イラン
情勢の悪化による燃料やその他商品市況の上昇を一部反映した」ことを挙げた。 一方で、 「同国内に
おける供給能力のひっ迫も想定以上にやや厳しい状況にある」 との見方を示し、「ＡＩ （人工知能）
関連投資が技術関連のインフレ圧力を招いているほか、 多くの財・サービスでインフレ圧力が依然
高い」 、 「多くの企業がコスト上昇圧力に直面するなか、財・サービス価格をすでに引き上げ、ない
し検討を行っており、インフレ率は 2027 年を通じて高止まりすると予想している」とした。その
結果、 「インフレ率が目標レンジに達するのは2027 年後半と従来の想定から約３四半期遅れる」と
して、 「インフレ期待への上振れリスクをもたらしている」との見方を示した。 
会合にあわせて公表した最新の『四半期金融政策報告』では、物価見通しが大幅に上方修正され

注２ 10 月28 日付レポート「豪ドル相場に持ち直しの兆し、RBA による追加利上げ観測が再燃」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666938.html）

インフレ率 コアインフレ率

### Page 4

4/6 
た。2026 年末のインフレ率は前年同期比＋4.5％と、 ８月会合時点の見通し （同＋3.6％） から大幅
に上方修正された。コアインフレ率についても、2027 年半ばの見通しを前年同期比＋3.5％と、８
月会合時点 （同＋3.0％） から上方修正した。 そのうえで、 ＲＢＡはインフレ率が目標レンジの上限
である３％以下となる時期を 2027 年末近くまで後ずれすると見込んでおり、目標レンジの中央値
である 2.5％への収束も2028 年半ばになるとの見方を示している。 加えて、 原油高などに伴う企業
のコスト増加が販売価格や賃金設定に波及し、 家計や企業のインフレ期待が高止まりするリスクに
も警戒感を示した。 
一方、金融環境については「年明け以降の利上げにより引き締まった」との見方を示した。その
うえで、 「経済は減速し、不動産価格も大幅に下落し、失業率も上昇している 」として、先行きは
「向こう１年で生産の伸びは一段と鈍化が見込まれ、 生産能力への圧力が緩和され、 インフレ率を
目標レンジに戻す一助になることが期待される」 とした。 また、 「金利上昇や不動産価格・取引高の
下落は、 不動産投資や家計消費を下押しする」 とする一方、 「データセンター関連投資を含む、 企業
部門による設備投資は当面堅調な推移が見込まれ」 、 「雇用も増加が見込まれるが、 失業率は８月会
合時点の想定より大幅に上昇する」との見通しを示した。 
ただし、 実体経済や物価見通しについては 「依然として不確実性が高い」 として、 「イラン情勢の
行方は見通せず、インフレ率が想定を上回り、実体経済が想定を下回るシナリオも考えられる」と
し、「不確実性が長期化すれば、 世界経済や同国の経済成長率が低下する可能性がある」 とした。 一
方で、 「足元ではＡＩ関連投資による押し上げ効果がイラン情勢の悪影響を上回っており、主要貿
易相手国の景気は想定を上回る力強さが続いている 」ものの、 「同国では生産性の伸びの鈍化が潜
在成長率の重しとなり、 不動産市場の低迷の度合いや実体経済への影響にも不確実性が残る」 との
見方を示した。 
今回の決定については 「インフレ期待が高止まりするリスクが高まっている」 ほか、 「インフレ見
通しや上振れリスクを踏まえ、 目標レンジに戻すためにはさらなる引き締めが妥当と判断した」と
した。そのうえで、先行きも「インフレ率を持続的に目標レンジに戻すために必要な措置を引き続
き講じる」として、 「経済指標や見通し、リスクに関する評価を注視する」としつつ、 「政策対応は
情勢変化に対応できる態勢が整っており、 物価安定と完全雇用の実現というマンデートに注力する」
との考えを示した。ただし、９月会合では追加利上げに言及する文言が含まれていたものの、今回
はそうした文言は削除されるなど明確な方向感を示されなかった。

【ブロック総裁は過去の利上げ効果を見極める姿勢を強調、追加利上げには慎重な構えをみせる】 
一方で、 今回の会合後に実施されたブロック総裁の記者会見では、 先行きの金融政策運営につい
て慎重な姿勢がうかがわれた。同氏は、今回の利上げ決定について、原油価格の上昇による直接的
なインフレへの対応ではなく、 従来からの国内における需給ひっ迫や、 エネルギー価格の上昇が幅
広い物価に波及する二次的な影響を抑制することへの対応と説明した。 ただし、 年明け以降に実施
した５回の利上げのうち、 直近の利上げの効果は実体経済に十分に浸透していないとして、 今後公
表される経済指標を見極める必要性を強調した。 今回の会合で据え置きを支持した理事についても、

### Page 5

5/6 
不動産市場の調整や失業率の上昇、 賃金上昇率が加速していないことなどを重視し、 金融引き締め
の効果を確認するために時間を置くべきだと判断したことを明らかにした。 
事実、 足元の国内景気にはこれまでの金融引き締めの影響が広がりをみせている。 ９月の失業率
は 4.7％と上昇傾向にあるとともに、 不動産価格の下落や住宅ローン需要が減少していることなど、
金利上昇に敏感な分野で調整圧力が強まる動きが確認されている。 ＲＢＡは今回公表した最新の経
済見通しにおいて、2027 年末の実質GDP 成長率を前年同期比＋1.3％と、 ８月会合時点の見通し （同
＋1.6％）から下方修正している。さらに、失業率も2028 年末に 5.1％まで上昇すると予想してお
り、 ８月会合時点の見通し （4.8％） から上方修正している。 同氏は、 中心的な見通しでは景気後退
を想定していないとしつつ、経済成長率の大幅な鈍化や家計消費の弱さが続くとの認識を示した。 
注目されるのは、 前述した通りＲＢＡが声明文から、 必要に応じて追加利上げを行うとの明示的
な表現を削除したことである。 同氏は、 これを金融政策の方向転換を示すものと受け止めるべきで
はないと説明しており、追加利上げの可能性を排除してはいない。ただし、理事会内において判断
が分かれたことに加え、 これまでの利上げによる累積的な影響を考慮すれば、 ＲＢＡが今後も断続
的に利上げを実施する可能性は低下したと考えられる。 当面は現行水準で政策金利を据え置き、 物
価や雇用、不動産市場の動向を慎重に見極める展開が予想される。一方で、原油価格の高止まりや
インフレ期待の上昇が続けば、2027 年前半に追加利上げに動く可能性も残る。 反対に、 不動産市場
の調整が深刻化するとともに、 個人消費や雇用の悪化が想定以上に進む場合には、 金融引き締めの
長期化を見直す必要性が強まるとみられる。

【豪ドル相場は利上げ決定後に弱含む動き、先行きも当面は上値の重い展開が続く可能性】 
金融市場では、前述の通り今回の利上げが事前に相当程度織り込まれていたこともあり、決定直
後の豪ドル相場は対米ドルで下落した
（図３）。 声明において、追加利上げを明
示する文言が削除されたことに加え、理
事会内 部において 据え置きを支持する
意見がみられたことも、追加的な金融引
き締めの 実施に対する市場の見方を後
退させたと考えられる。 今後の豪ドル相
場を巡っては、ＲＢＡの政策金利が高水
準で維持されることが一定の下支え要
因になると見込まれるものの、 国内景気
の減速に伴って追加利上げ期待が後退
すれば、豪ドル高圧力は限定的となる可能性がある。 
さらに、豪ドル相場は国内の金融政策だけでなく、米国の金融政策や中国景気、国際商品市況、
世界的な投資家のリスク選好の影響を受けやすい。 仮にＲＢＡが追加利上げに踏み切る一方、 米国
の金融引き締め姿勢が弱まれば、 米国とオーストラリアの間の金利差の変化が豪ドル相場を押し上

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。

げる可能性がある。しかし、米国でもインフレ圧力が根強く、金融引き締めの長期化観測が強まる
場合には、米ドル高が豪ドルの上値を抑えるとみられる。また、中国景気の減速や中東情勢の悪化
によるリスク回避姿勢の強まりも、豪ドルの下押し要因となり得る。こうした点を踏まえると、豪
ドル相場は短期的に一進一退の展開となるとともに、 明確な上昇基調への転換には、 豪州の景気減
速への懸念後退や米国との金融政策の方向性の違いが一段と明確になることが必要であろう。

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Bank of Thailand (BOT) (October 28)

We expect the BOT to maintain its policy 
rate at 1.00% at its 28 October meeting, 
extending its pause for a fourth 
consecutive decision as it balances 
inflationary pressures against uneven 
and subdued economic growth. Our view 
is aligned with fixed market income 
pricing. Headline inflation rose anew to 
2.8% yoy in September 2026, driven by 
higher energy, food, and core price 
increases, but remained within the upper 
end of the central bank’s 1 -3% target 
range. As the BOT is still likely to look 
through what it views as temporary, 
supply-driven inflationary pressu res, it 
will likely refrain from tightening policy, 
and keep interest rates accommodative 
to support the soft and uneven growth 
cycle.

European Central Bank (October 29)

We expect the ECB to extend its pause, 
keeping the deposit facility rate at 2.5%. 
Market participants will instead focus on 
the central bank’s assessment of rising 
French and Italian bond yields and 
widening spreads versus Bunds. While 
we do not expect dir ect references to 
individual countries, the ECB is likely to 
signal that recent developments in 
France reflect the country’s weaker 
fiscal and debt profile rather than a 
broader systemic concern. Policymakers 
will seek to differentiate between a 
localised bond sell -off and a Eurozone -
wide market disruption, justifying their 
decision to monitor developments rather 
than implement specific measures at this 
juncture. Meanwhile, inflation continues 
to rise, with little relief expected in 
upcoming readings given elevated 
global fuel prices. 
Bank of Japan (October 30)

The Bank of Japan is expected to keep 
the overnight call rate unchanged at 1.25% 
at this meeting, pausing after the 25bp 
hike in September. The BOJ is likely to 
assess the impact of the previous rate 
hike before taking further action. The 
recent stabilisat ion of the yen also 
reduces the urgency of another hike at 
this meeting.

The tightening bias should remain intact. 
Growth and inflation remain broadly on 
track, leaving room for the BOJ to revise 
up its FY2026 –FY2027 GDP and CPI 
forecasts. Wage growth has also 
remained strong, with Rengo reportedly 
targeting wage increases of 6% in 2027, 
reinforcing the BOJ’s confidence that 
underlying inflation can be sustained 
around its 2% target. We continue to 
expect the policy rate to rise to 1.50% in 
December 2026 or January 2027, 
followed by a further increase to 1.75% 
by 2Q 2027.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Hong Kong SAR

Export growth is expected to accelerate 
from 53.0% yoy in August to 55.0% in 
September, in line with the pickup in 
China’s export growth from 25.0% to 
36.3%. As a key re-export hub for China,

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 23, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

Hong Kong should benefit from robust 
AI-related electronics trade, with 
electrical machinery, equipment and 
parts exports to Hong Kong accounting 
for 20.6% of China’s total exports. 
Import growth is also expected to remain 
firm, supported by resilient expo rt 
orders and gradually improving 
investment sentiment, sustaining 
demand for intermediate and capital 
goods.

Singapore

Singapore’s industrial production (IP) 
likely remained in expansion for a 13th 
consecutive month in September 2026, 
although we expect growth to moderate 
to 7.6% yoy from August’s strong 15.4% 
yoy pace. IP growth continued to be 
underpinned by the electronics and 
precision engineering clusters, which 
has been key drivers of Singapore’s 
manufacturing strength, reflecting 
sustained demand for artificial 
intelligence-related hardware. However, 
performance across clusters remained 
uneven, with chemicals and biomedical 
manufacturing likely contracting, and 
tempering overall gains.

South Korea

The preliminary estimate for 3Q26 GDP 
is likely to show growth remaining solid 
at 3.2% yoy , slightly below 3.7% in 2Q 
and 3.8% in 1Q. Nominal GDP growth is 
likely to remain in double digits, 
significantly outpacing real GDP growth, 
thanks to improved terms of trade amid 
rising memory export prices. By 
expenditure, exports picked up in 3Q in 
both nominal and real terms, supported 
by stronger semiconductor shipments. 
Investment also appeared robust, as 
reflected in the equipment investment 
estimation index and machinery order 
index. Private consumption, however, 
appeared to weaken amid the sharp 
KOSPI correction since July following  
the introduction of leveraged ETF 
regulations. Our full -year GDP growth 
forecasts of 3.5% for 2026 and 2.4% for 
2027 remain on track.

Taiwan

The preliminary estimate for 3Q GDP is 
likely to show growth remaining strong 
at 12 –13% yoy , close to 12.9% in 2Q, 
albeit below the peak of 15.4% in 1Q. 
Exports maintained strong double -digit 
growth in 3Q (44.6% yoy  in nominal 
terms and 21.1% in real terms), supported 
by robust shipments of semiconductors, 
servers and other AI -related products. 
Machinery investment also sustained 
strong double-digit growth, underpinned 
by higher capacity utilisation in the ICT 
sector and a resulting increase in capital 
goods imports. Private consumption 
appeared to have strengthened further, 
supported by stock market gains, 
improving wage growth, and the 
associated wealth and income effects. 
Construction investment remained the 
weak s pot, weighed down by elevated 
inventories in the new-home market and 
the ongoing need for inventory digestion. 
We maintain our 2026 and 2027 GDP 
growth forecasts at 11.6% and 5.6%, 
respectively, with risks tilted to the 
upside.

光寶科 2301 TT (電子-零組件) 
高壓放量時程仍待觀察 
投資與評價 
1. 3Q26 受惠 AI 伺服器電源及 Cloud 需求成長 ， 營收季增8.9%， 毛利率25.9%，
EPS 3.27 元創單季新高。預估 4Q26 Cloud 業務持續成長，惟 PC 及消費性
電子需求疲弱，EPS 2.96 元。 
2. 2027 年成長動能來自 ASIC 8.5kW PSU/BBU、NVIDIA 110kW Power Shelf
及新客戶導入，惟記憶體缺料、資料中心建置遞延及800VDC 放量時程延後，
限制獲利成長，預估 2026/27 年 EPS 10.84/11.49 元。 
3. 目前股價為 240.5 元(約 20.9x FY27E 修正後 EPS)，於近半年本益比區間
12~26 倍中間偏上位置 。 考量高毛利率產品的放量時間仍須等待 ， 將2027 年
目標本益比由 22 倍下調至 21 倍 ， 目標價從先前目標價278 元(22x FY27E 修
正前 EPS)下調至 242 元(21x FY27E 修正後 EPS)。

重點更新 
3Q26 AI 電源占比持續提升 
3Q26 營收 574.03 億元（+8.9% QoQ、+27.9% YoY） ，低於原估3.1%，受惠 AI
伺服器電源及 Cloud 業務持續成長，其中 Cloud/AIoT 營收 337.53 億元（+57% 
YoY） ，占整體營收59%，惟 PC 及消費性電子需求相對疲弱。毛利 148.67 億元
（+3.6% QoQ） ，毛利率25.9%（-1.3ppts QoQ） ，高於原估1.9ppts，主要受惠
高附加價值 AI 電源比重提升及營運效率改善 ； 營業費用及其他營業損益淨額62.11
億元（+1.1% QoQ） ，占營收10.8%，營業利益 86.55 億元（+5.5% QoQ） ，營益
率 15.1%（-0.5ppts QoQ） ，高於原估3.1ppts。業外收益 11.42 億元，稅後淨利
74.29 億元，EPS 3.27 元創單季新高。整體而言，光寶在機種的晶片短缺供應影響
較小，支撐獲利持續成長。

2027 年 800VDC 放量部分遞延壓抑獲利 
預估 2027 年營收 2,529.25 億元（+19.8% YoY） ，較原估下修5.0%，成長主要來
自 CSP ASIC 平台 8.5kW PSU/BBU、NVIDIA Vera Rubin 18.3kW PSU與 110kW 
Power Shelf 持續出貨，以及新客戶與低軌衛星電源需求增加。惟記憶體缺料、客
戶資料中心建置遞延，加上 800VDC Power Rack 仍處於送樣驗證及初期導入階
段，較大規模貢獻可能延至 2028 年。預估毛利率 25.4%（+0.1ppts YoY） ，營業
費用 334.91 億元（+34.7% YoY） ，營業利益307.86 億元（+7.6% YoY） ，歸母稅
後淨利 266.08 億元 ，EPS 11.49 元 。 光寶已由單一PSU 延伸至 BBU、Power Shelf
及 Power Rack 系統整合，但高毛利新產品放量時程仍須觀察。

報告內容 
3Q26 AI 電源與 Cloud 需求推升獲利創高 
3Q26 營收 574.03 億元（+8.9% QoQ、+27.9% YoY） ，低於原估3.1%，受惠 AI 伺服器電源及 Cloud 業務持續成
長，其中 Cloud/AIoT 營收 337.53 億元（+57% YoY） ，占整體營收59%，惟 PC 及消費性電子需求相對疲弱。毛
利 148.67 億元（+3.6% QoQ） ，毛利率25.9%（-1.3ppts QoQ） ，高於原估1.9ppts，主要受惠高附加價值 AI 電
源比重提升及營運效率改善；營業費用及其他營業損益淨額 62.11 億元（+1.1% QoQ） ，占營收10.8%，營業利益
86.55 億元（+5.5% QoQ） ，營益率15.1%（-0.5ppts QoQ） ，高於原估3.1ppts。業外收益 11.42 億元，稅後淨
利 74.29 億元 ，EPS 3.27 元創單季新高 。整體而言 ， 光寶在既有機種的晶片短缺供應影響較小 ， 支撐獲利持續成長 。

4Q26 Cloud 出貨維持成長，非 Cloud 需求限制獲利 
預估 4Q26 營收 576.63 億元（+0.5% QoQ） ，低於原估3.0%，雖受惠既有 CSP ASIC 平台 8.5kW PSU/BBU 持續
出貨，但預料規格較高的 18.3kW PSU、110kW Power Shelf 將因客戶步調調整而減緩，且 PC、消費性電子需求
疲弱 ， 加上客戶記憶體缺料及年底工作天數減少 ， 使整體營收成長相對有限 。 預估毛利148.52 億元 （-0.1% QoQ），
毛利率 25.8%（-0.1ppts QoQ） ， 高於原估0.9ppts； 營業費用72.08 億元 （+16.0% QoQ） ， 費用率12.5%（+1.7ppts 
QoQ） ，營業利益76.44 億元（-11.7% QoQ） ，營益率13.3%（-1.8ppts QoQ） 。業外收益10.98 億元，稅後淨利
68.59 億元，EPS 2.96 元低於前季。其中 800VDC Power Rack 於 4Q26 以樣機出貨及客戶驗證為主，尚非主要營
收來源。

### Page 3

2026 年 AI 電源與低軌衛星帶動獲利創高 
預估 2026 年營收 2,111.77 億元（+27.1% YoY） ，較原估下修1.7%，雖受惠 AI 伺服器高瓦數 PSU、BBU、Power 
Shelf 及低軌衛星電源需求成長，AI 相關營收占比可望超過 30%，其中 8.5kW PSU/BBU 及 110kW Power Shelf已
進入量產階段 ， 低軌衛星電源出貨亦呈倍數成長 。 惟PC 及消費性電子需求疲弱 ， 限制整體營收增幅 。 預估毛利534.70
億元 （+40.6% YoY） ，毛利率25.3%（+2.4ppts YoY） ，高於原估0.8ppts；營業費用 248.68 億元 （+16.7% YoY），
費用率 11.8%（-1.0ppts YoY） ，營業利益286.01 億元（+70.9% YoY） ，營益率13.5%（+3.5ppts YoY） 。業外收
益 42.77 億元，稅前淨利 328.78 億元，歸母稅後淨利 251.80 億元，EPS 10.84 元創歷史新高。

2027 年新平台持續導入，800VDC 放量遞延限制獲利 
預估 2027 年營收 2,529.25 億元 （+19.8% YoY） ， 較原估下修5.0%，雖有成長動能來自既有 CSP ASIC 平台 8.5kW 
PSU/BBU 持續放量、NVIDIA Vera Rubin相關 18.3kW PSU 與 110kW Power Shelf出貨增加，以及新客戶導入與
低軌衛星電源規格升級。但 800VDC Power Rack 於 2027 年仍以客戶驗證及新平台初期出貨為主，較大規模貢獻
可能遞延至 2028 年，且高壓晶片行業的市場供給仍然緊缺，加上客戶端部分長短料與資料中心建置進度限制，影
響營收成長。預估毛利 642.77 億元（+20.2% YoY） ，毛利率25.4%（+0.1ppts YoY） ，低於原估0.1ppts；營業費
用 334.91 億元（+34.7% YoY） ，費用率13.2%（+1.5ppts YoY） ，營業利益307.86 億元（+7.6% YoY） ，營益率
12.2%（-1.4ppts YoY） 。業外收益25.51 億元，歸母稅後淨利 266.08 億元，EPS 11.49 元持續創下歷史新高。

評價 
新平台持續成長，800VDC 放量時程仍待觀察 
光寶科受惠 AI 伺服器電源功率提升 、BBU 滲透率增加及 Power Shelf 出貨成長 ，ASIC 8.5kW PSU/BBU 與 NVIDIA 
110kW Power Shelf已進入量產 ， 支撐2026–2027 年成長 。 惟記憶體缺料 、 客戶資料中心建置遞延 ， 以及800VDC 
Power Rack 放量時間可能遞延，限制短期獲利成長，因此調整2026/2027 年 EPS 至 10.84/11.49 元，分別較原估
上修 7.8%／下修 9.0%。考量 AI 電源系統整合能力持續提升，但新平台量產進度仍有不確定性，目前光寶科股價
為 240.5 元(約 20.9x FY27E 修正後 EPS)，於近半年本益比區間 12~26 倍中間偏上位置。考量高毛利率產品的放量
時間仍須等待，將 2027 年目標本益比由 22 倍下調至 21 倍，目標價從先前目標價 278 元(22x FY27E 修正前 EPS)
下調至 242 元(21x FY27E 修正後 EPS)。

產業概況 
高電壓直流未來將成主軸，從 48/50 V 架構走向 ±400 V/800 V DC 的架構升級，以降低配電銅損、縮短轉換鏈，
支撐高功率 AI 機櫃。800 V DC 配電正成為新藍圖的一環。機櫃級功率已把 500 kW+ 機櫃、乃至 MW-class 機
櫃，對 PSU 密度、效率、散熱與安全冗餘標準更高。 除了既有 ORV3 生態，OCP 官方也釋出 High Power Rack 
v3（HPRv3）50 V 側邊供電設計，凸顯在相容 48/50 V 負載同時，為高功率機櫃優化匯流排與供電路徑。 Open 
Compute Project Power shelf 功率級距包括了 72–100 kW 等級 power shelf、2U 72 kW BBU，效率達到 97.5–
~98%。 配電層次上移：從 PSU 單體到 rack/row-level 電源子系統（含 power shelf、bus-bar、直流配電、儲
能/BBU、監控韌體一體化） ，以模組化、可熱抽換、快速部署滿足 AI 機房擴張。 電源×冷卻協同設計：高密度液
冷標準化與 800 V DC 配電並行，被視為 AI 資料中心的兩大支柱。 
       圖 3. 光寶機架電源發展藍圖

資料來源: 光寶科, 國泰證期研究部整理 
公司簡介 
光寶科技股份有限公司成立於 1990 年，2024 年產品分為三大業務，光電部門佔 20%、消費電子佔 43%、雲端及
物聯網部門佔 37%。過去在 2022 年集團合併旗下四家公司，光寶電子、旭麗公司、源興科技、致福公司，以源興
科技為存續公司，並更名為光寶科技迄今。公司為 LED、電源供應器、機殼、影像產品生產之領導廠商。在光電產
品-LED 光電半導體同業包括了歐司朗 、 日亞化學 、 億光電子 。 雲端 、 資訊與消費性電子產品-電源供應器 台達電子 、
康舒科技、群光電等。三大事業部如下:

### Page 5

圖 4. 光寶科產品組合

資料來源: 光寶科，國泰證期研究部整理

光電半導體: LED 包含可見光和不可見光兩大類 LED 元件產品，此外亦包含戶外照明模組。應用於各資訊產品、消
費性電子、車用照明及 UV 殺菌等領域。在高效率 LED 光源產品方面，汽車照明客戶涵蓋歐洲、美洲與日本等一線
電動車車廠。不可見光 LED 產品涵蓋光耦合器、紅外線感測元件、環境光源感應器等。光耦合器目前為全球光耦合
器出貨量第一。紅外線感測裝置則應用於生物辨識及檢測、AR/VR、移動機器人等相關領域。 
車用電子: 擁有超過 40 年之汽車電子產品開發生產經驗，在車燈模組方面，生產各項車內外 LED 照明模組產品，
從車內照明如氛圍燈，到車外照明包含智慧頭燈控制模組、前後霧燈模組、轉向燈車燈模組等已運用於全球知名汽
車大廠產品中。車用鏡頭模組、感測元件等則廣泛應用於汽車智慧化裝置中，ADAS 輔助駕駛系統、DMS 駕駛監測
系統等。 
伺服器與電源應用: 於 96 年與力信興業合併，生產之電源供應器有超過三成為高階電源管理系統，應用於網通與伺
服器等企業雲端運算高階產品。其電源已開發 750W Desktop 電源供應器, 採用第三代半導體元件與搭配特殊散熱
結構，達成高效鈦金高功率密度(26.9 W/in³)與無風扇設計，1,800W 全數位控制工作站電源供應器，其功率密度達
37 W/in³，最新伺服器用 PSU 已達 100 W/in³ 。雲端運算電源方面，除提供伺服器電源供應器，亦提供大型資料
中心客戶高階伺服器電源管理系統，電源管理系統除了整合電源與電池儲能供電系統外，亦結合液冷式散熱解決方
案，同時整合光寶軟體、韌體及硬體開發能力。伺服器電源方面推出符合 OCP 標準的 10kW, 48V 輸出，高效率
(>97.5%)資料數據中心專用的伺服器電源 ， 適用於最新21 吋開放式機架系統 。 更進一步開發整機櫃500kW 系統 ，
滿足下一代 AI 伺服器高功耗能源需求。在液冷解決方案和 AI 應用方面，已完成：(1)換熱器：600kW 液對液列間
型 CDU 和 60kW 液對氣邊櫃型熱交換器兩大產品，並被列入 Nvidia RVL (2)水歧管：標準型 27OU / 48RU 水歧管
已列入 Nvidia RVL (3)機櫃：21"和 19" (MGX) 款分別列入 Nvidia NVL，也為雲端服務供應商和企業客戶提供定製
款機櫃。

Document 549: ブラジルの「トリプル安」は一過性の混乱にとどまるか ～ルラ氏の逆転勝利で財政懸念が再燃、硬直的な歳出構造が信認回復の足かせに～

Source PDF: all report pdf/ブラジルの「トリプル安」は一過性の混乱にとどまるか ～ルラ氏の逆転勝利で財政懸念が再燃、硬直的な歳出構造が信認回復の足かせに～.pdf
Report source: DLRI

Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

World Trends

2026 年10 月 27 日

1/5 
ブラジルの「トリプル安」は一過性の混乱にとどまるか 
～ルラ氏の逆転勝利で財政懸念が再燃、硬直的な歳出構造が信認回復の足かせに～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ ブラジルでは、10 月 25 日の大統領選挙の決選投票において現職のルラ大統領が逆転勝利を
収めたことを受けて、 翌26 日の金融市場では株式、 通貨レアル、 国債が同時に売られる 「ト
リプル安」に直面した。背景には、右派フラビオ氏の勝利による財政健全化や金利低下への
期待を過度に織り込んだ取引が巻き戻されたことに加え、ルラ政権下で財政健全化の取り組
みが遅れるとの懸念がある。 
○ 同国では、政府債務の大きさや義務的経費の割合の高さが財政運営の制約となるなか、レア
ル安によるインフレ圧力の高まりが中銀の追加利下げ余地を狭めるとともに、金利上昇が景
気や財政を一段と圧迫するリスクが懸念される。一方、十分な外貨準備など対外ショックに
対する一定の耐性を有するほか、当局も市場安定化策を講じており、過度な相場変動が落ち
着く余地はある。 
○ ただし、市場の信認回復には具体的な財政健全化への取り組みが不可欠である。今後は、次
期政権の経済チームの人事や2027 年度予算の内容、 議会との合意形成を通じて、 財政健全化
に向けた道筋を示せるかが重要となる。

### Page 2

2/5 
【ルラ氏の逆転勝利を受けて、金融市場においてブラジル資産を売却する動きが広がる】 
ブラジルでは、10 月 25 日に実施された大統領選挙の決選投票において、左派ＰＴ（労働者党）
から出馬した現職のルラ大統領が、 右派
ＰＬ（自由党）のフラビオ・ボルソナロ
上院議員を破り、再選を決めた（注１）。
ブラジル高等選挙裁判所 （ＴＳＥ） が公
表した最終結果によれば、 ルラ氏の得票
率は 51.79％、フラビオ氏は 48.21％と
なった。これを受けて、翌 26 日の金融
市場では、株式、通貨レアル、国債のす
べてに売り圧力が強まった（図１）。 株式
市場では、 主要株価指数（ボベスパ指数）
が前営業日比で一時▲17％以上下落し、
取引を一時停止するサーキットブレーカーが発動された。 為替市場においてもレアルが大きく下落
するとともに、 債券市場では国債利回りや金利先物市場での金利上昇が進むなど、 「トリプル安」 に
直面した。

【右派政権への交代を過度に織り込んだ取引の巻き戻しが、相場変動を増幅させた】 
市場が大幅に変動した背景には、決選投票の前に積み上がった政権交代への期待が剥落したこと
がある。10 月４日の第１回投票では、事前予想に反してフラビオ氏が首位となった（注２） 。これ
を受けて、その後の市場では、同氏の勝利によって財政健全化が進み、将来的な金利低下につなが
るとの期待が強まる動きがみられた （注３）。 決選投票直前には、 ボベスパ指数は第１回投票前に比
べて約７％、レアルも対ドルで約５％上昇していた。 
投資家の間では、フラビオ氏の勝利を受けた財政規律の強化や経済改革が進む可能性を織り込み、
株式やレアルを買うとともに、金利低下を見込む取引を進めていた。しかし、ルラ氏の逆転勝利に
よってこうした前提が崩れ、複数の市場で同時に持ち高を調整する動きが強まったと考えられる。
よって、現職の続投という結果にもかかわらず、市場が政権交代を強く見込んでいたことで、大幅
な価格調整に繋がった。今回の下落幅を考える際には、ルラ政権の政策に対する評価に加え、選挙
前の取引が特定の結果に偏っていた可能性を考慮する必要がある。このため、市場では右派政権に
よる政策転換への期待が過度に先行していたと考えられる。

注１ 10 月26 日付レポート「ブラジル大統領選、ルラ氏が逆転勝利も政権運営には難路」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666866.html） 
注２ 10 月５日付レポート「ブラジル大統領選は決選投票へ、フラビオ氏が首位」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666284.html） 
注３ 10 月６日付レポート「金融市場はブラジルの右派回帰を好感、株価・レアルともに上昇」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666333.html）

### Page 3

3/5

【市場が抱く懸念には、公的財務の大きさに加え、 硬直的な歳出増による財政運営の難しさがある】 
市場がルラ氏の再選に厳しい反応を示した背景には、社会給付などの拡充が続くことにより、財
政健全化の取り組みが 遅れるとの懸念
がある。2026 年８月末時点における一般
政府の債務残高 （グロス値）はＧＤＰ比
82.9％に達している。さらに、ここ数年
の同国では、 インフレによる高金利が常
態化するなか、利払い負担の大きさが財
政運営を難しくしている。さらに、 近年
は司法判断に基づく支払債務 （プレカト
リオス）の増大も歳出を圧迫しており、
財政運営の硬直性を一段と強める要因
となっている。 
こうした状況では、 新たな恒久歳出に見合う財源を確保できるかが重要になる。 財政の持続可能
性に対する懸念が強まれば、投資家は国債の保有により高い利回りを求める。この結果、政府の新
規調達や借換えに伴う負担が増え、財政健全化を一段と困難にする可能性がある。株式、通貨、債
券が同時に売られたことは、 将来の政策運営に対する不確実性が、 ブラジル資産全体に求められる
リスクの上乗せ幅を拡大させたと解釈できる。 
財政運営の硬直性は、2027 年度予算案において、 基礎的歳出に占める義務的経費の割合が91.7％
に上ることにも現れている。このため、 裁量的な歳出削減による財政健全化の実現のハードルは極
めて高いと考えられる。今後は、政府が掲げる財政目標に加え、前述したプレカトリオスなど例外
措置を含めた実際の歳出、恒久財源の確保、債務残高の見通しを確認することが重要になる。ルラ
政権が主導する社会政策の効果を見極めつつ、給付の対象や制度設計、税制優遇、支出の優先順位
を見直すことができるかが、財政運営への信認を左右することが予想される。

【レアル安が利下げ余地を狭める一方、金利上昇が景気と財政を圧迫する懸念も残る】 
今回の混乱が、 金融政策の先行きに影響を与える可能性にも注意が必要である。 中銀は９月の定
例会合（COPOM）において、政策金利である Selic を 13.75％に引き下げた（注４）。一方で、会合
後に公表された声明においては、 持続的な通貨下落やインフレ期待の不安定化、 消費刺激策などを
物価の上振れリスクに挙げるとともに、 財政政策が金融政策や資産価格に及ぼす影響を注視する姿
勢を示した。

注４ ９月17 日付レポート「ブラジル中銀は５会合連続の利下げも、緩和継続には慎重姿勢」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/665843.html）

### Page 4

4/5 
足元では、イラン情勢の悪化を受けた原油高などを背景に、鈍化基調が続いてきたインフレ率が
再び加速に転じる兆しがみられる（図
３） 。 こうしたなか、レアル安の動きが長
期化すれば、 輸入品や輸入原材料の価格
上昇を通じて、 物価への押し上げ圧力が
強まることが予想される。 さらに、 企業
や家計 部門における インフレ予想が上
方修正されれば、 中銀が追加利下げを進
める余地は狭まる。次回の COPOM は 11
月３～４日に予定されており、 選挙後の
為替変動や期待インフレの動きをどう
評価するかが注目される。 中銀として
は、レアル安の継続性のほか、 物価への波及、 国内需要の減速などを総合的に判断する必要がある。 
実体経済への影響としては、 企業と家計の資金調達環境が厳しくなることが懸念される。 企業の
借入費用や債務の借換え負担が増えれば、 利益や設備投資に下押し圧力がかかる。家計向け融資の
金利上昇は、 住宅や耐久消費財の需要を抑える要因となる。こうした金融環境の悪化が長期化すれ
ば、 景気減速による税収の伸び悩みと利払い負担の増加が重なり、 財政運営をさらに難しくするこ
とも考えられる。 財政運営に対する懸念が金利を押し上げるとともに、 その金利上昇が景気と財政
を圧迫するという循環を抑えられるかが、今後の重要な課題となる。

【当局の市場安定化策と対外面での「緩衝材」はあるが、財政運営への信認回復が不可欠】 
市場の混乱を受けて、 当局は国債市場の安定化に向けた対応を進めている。26 日には固定利付国
債や物価連動国債の買い戻しを実施するとともに、27 日に予定していた物価連動国債と29 日の固
定利付国債の入札を中止する一方、27 日の政策金利連動国債の入札は維持した。これらの措置は、
売り注文が集中した市場において買い手を確保する一方、 急激な価格変動を抑えることに役立つと
考えられる。ただし、その効果を持続させるには、中期的な財政改善策を示すとともに、投資家が
国債を保有しやすい環境を整えることが必要になる。

### Page 5

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
5/5 
一方で、対外的な観点でみればブラジルには「緩衝材」が存在している。９月末時点における外
貨準備高は3,054 億ドルに上る。 この水
準は、ＩＭＦ（国際通貨基金）が国際金
融市場の動揺への耐性の有無の基準と
して示すＡＲＡ（適正水準評価）に照ら
して 「適正 （100～150％） 」 の域内にとど
まる（図４） 。そのうえ、９月までの 12
ヵ月累計の経常赤字は600 億ドルである
のに対し、対内直接投資の純流入は 883
億ドルとなっている。 これらの数字は選
挙前までの実績である ことに注意が必
要であるものの、 ブラジルが市場の急変
に対応するうえで一定の基盤を持つことを示唆している。ＩＭＦが７月に公表したⅣ条協議 （対ブ
ラジル審査） レポートにおいても、 変動相場制と十分な外貨準備が外部ショックを吸収する手段に
なると評価している。先行きについては、こうした基盤を踏まえつつ、財政不安と高金利が長期化
するリスクを見極める必要がある。

【先行きは次期政権における経済チームの人事、2027 年予算、議会との合意形成に要注意】 
今後注目すべき点は、 次期政権の経済チームの人選と、 大統領が財政改善策をどの程度政治的に
支えるかである。財務相などの人事は、政策運営の方向を示す材料となるとともに、その後に示さ
れる具体的な歳出・歳入措置も重要になる。そのうえで、2027 年予算案の修正、 新たな給付や支援
策の財源、義務的経費や税制優遇の見直しなどが、信認回復の判断材料となり得る。 
議会との関係も政策の実行可能性を左右することは避けられない。 次期下院においては、フラビ
オ氏が所属するＰＬが 121 議席と最大勢力となり、ＰＴを中心とする政党連合が 88 議席を獲得す
る見通しである。 ルラ政権としては、 予算や制度改正を進めるために他党との合意形成を図る必要
がある。右派勢力の影響力は、急激な歳出拡大を抑える要因となり得る。一方で、予算配分や税制
を巡る交渉が難航すれば、 財政健全化策の成立も遅れる可能性がある。 議会構成だけで財政規律の
改善を判断せず、 具体的な政策についてどのような合意が成立するかを確認することが重要である。 
現時点においては、 選挙前に積み上がった取引の巻き戻しが一巡すれば、 急激な値動きが落ち着
く余地はあると見込まれる。 しかし、 選挙前の相場を支えていた財政改善や金利低下への期待を取
り戻すには、政策面での裏付けが不可欠である。ルラ政権にとっては、有権者が期待する生活改善
の要求に応える一方、歳出の優先順位と財源を明確にしつつ、 政府債務の拡大を抑える道筋を示す
ことが求められる。

景気は弱い動き
中国景気は弱い動きが続いている。9月の製造業PMIは50.1と前月から上昇し、好不況の境目である50を3カ月ぶりに上
回った。構成指数をみると、生産は大きく上昇した一方、就業人員の低下が全体の改善ペースを抑制した。非製造業PMI
は50.2と3カ月ぶりに50を上回ったが、過去と比べると低水準で推移している。なお、2026年4～6月期の実質GDP成長
率は前年比＋4.3%と伸びが鈍化し、季節調整済み前期比（年率換算）も＋3.6%と政府目標の「4.5～5%」を下回った。

6 Mitsubishi UFJ Research and Consulting
9月の米中首脳会談は一定の成果も、中心は対話継続の確認
9月24日、米国・ワシントンで米中首脳会談が実施された。5月の会談で打ち出された「建設的戦略安定関係」の構築を再
確認したほか、年内あと2回の会談の可能性が示唆されるなど、首脳間の対話の継続が確認された（下図）。
具体的には、米中双方が300億ドル相当の品目で関税引き下げに合意したほか、AI分野で対話枠組みを構築する方針で
合意するなど、個別分野では一定の進展が得られた。もっとも、総じてみると内容は概ね想定内であり、米中対立の全面
的な解消には至っていない。貿易戦争の休戦も今年11月までとされた期限が2カ月延長されたにとどまっており、今後も
両者間で対話を継続することを確認したことが最大の成果だったといえよう。
2026年9月の米中首脳会談における主な内容

貿易 一部関税引き下げと休戦延長 5月に設置した米中貿易委員会の運用を開始相互に300億ドル相当の非機微品目で関税引き下げに合意関税・輸出管理などを巡る休戦を2027年1月10日まで延長

輸出価格は上昇も、デフレ輸出への懸念はなお強い
中国の輸出価格が足元で上昇している（左図）。2023年以降、EVや太陽光パネル、鉄鋼を中心に輸出価格は下落傾向
にあり、廉価品の輸出が海外市場を席巻する「デフレ輸出」が国際問題となったが、2026年4月以降、状況が一変した。品
目別でみると、世界的に需給が逼迫している集積回路やPC、PC部品の価格上昇が顕著である。
もっとも、足元の価格上昇は一部の品目に偏っている。HSコード4桁で分類した全1,204品目のうち、足元でも5割近くで価
格が下落している（右図）。このため、デフレ輸出への懸念は依然として根強い。

輸出価格 輸出価格が前年から上昇／下落した品目の割合
（出所） 海関総署 （注） 前年から横ばい品目は下落品目の上にグレーで表示（毎月0.0 ～0.7% の割合）（出所） 海関総署
